  4. 0:40-0:45, file back. Close the folder and return it to your named spot on the portfolio shelf, credit roll and all. Crew collects the 3 pitch-rehearsal timers from the team tables back into the Day 9 box on the instructor table (prep pulled them from that box for the rehearsal; this is the crew step that returns them, so the next session's Day 9 prep finds them instead of an empty box). The folders sleep in the room tonight; Friday's prep pulls them for the gallery stations.

Finished early? Write the three-sentence version of your pitch for parents who arrive late to your station, on the inside front of your portfolio folder, labeled "Late arrivals." It files with the folder; Friday's prep stages the folder at your station, so the short version is where you stand.

Stuck? Start the credit roll with the failure, not the success. The failure is the part you remember best, and the page wants honesty first.

Tear here at pickup. Dinner-table card, one question for the ride home: Ask a grown-up: what is something they signed their name to and were proud to own?

Day 10 · project-block handout

The Final Polish

You need: your station, the gallery labels (first name only on every label, never your last name), your portfolio, the sticky-note pads at the gallery entrance. 45 minutes, same room, quiet voices: speak only to your partner or a station owner, task words only. The whole block is room-only; never goes home.

  1. 0:00-0:15, polish. Station polish: every label straight, every caption readable from six feet, every object in place.
  2. 0:15-0:30, stranger walk. Walk the gallery as a stranger, a sticky-note from the pads at the gallery entrance in hand. Leave one sticky-note fix at any station that needs it, including your own. Initial it so the station owner can find you. The station owner has the last word: apply the fix, fix it your own way, or tape the note under your station with your initials and "overruled, kept as is". If the note-writer thinks the owner is wrong, they say so once, at the station, in task words, and move on. A sticky-note gets one visit, never a debate, so no adult has to referee.
  3. 0:30-0:40, seal. Portfolio closed: final check against the ship list, credit roll signed by every name on it. Everything files into the folder except the credit roll, which mounts at your station with the badge tape from the Day 10 box, because it is the page a parent reads at pickup. Check the inside front of the folder for the three-sentence "Late arrivals" pitch, labeled "Late arrivals": Day 9's handout asked for it only as a finished-early step, so if it is not there, write it now, three sentences a parent with seconds only can hear, before the folder seals. Folder sealed.
  4. 0:40-0:45, read. Read your credit roll once, silently, the mounted page at your station. At pickup today, someone's parent reads it there. Make it honest. When the showcase ends, file the credit roll in your portfolio folder before the folder goes home in your backpack.

Finished early? Help a neighbor's station. The gallery is one product, and the weakest station is everyone's station.

Stuck? Stand six feet from your station and read every caption aloud. Whatever you stumble on is what needs the fix.

Tear here at pickup. Dinner-table card, one question for the ride home: Ask someone at your showcase station tonight: what did you make today that a machine could not?

Printing spec: one page each, 12pt minimum, camp logo in the header, junior and senior variants marked in the margin. The dinner-table conversation card prints as a perforated tear-off strip at the bottom of each day's handout sheet, so no separate card stock is needed; the strip tears off at pickup for the ride home. The ten questions are fixed for the session, each written to pair with the day's weakness:

  1. Day 1. Ask a grown-up: when has a computer been confidently wrong in their job?
  2. Day 2. Ask a grown-up: what does a camera not see that your eyes do?
  3. Day 3. Ask a grown-up: what is a fact they were told that turned out wrong, and how did they find out?
  4. Day 4. Ask a grown-up: when did a measurement change their mind?
  5. Day 5. Ask a grown-up: what is something a machine got wrong in their work, and who fixed it?
  6. Day 6. Ask a grown-up: what is a question only a person could answer for them, never a chatbot?
  7. Day 7. Ask a grown-up: what physical task looks easy and is actually hard?
  8. Day 8. Ask a grown-up: what is the difference between "correct" and "good" in their work?
  9. Day 9. Ask a grown-up: what is something they signed their name to and were proud to own?
  10. Day 10. Ask someone at your showcase station tonight: what did you make today that a machine could not?

The three Day 1 AI Fail binders (one per team table) are 1-inch 3-ring binders bought once as reusable shop stock; the twelve printed real-world blunders per binder print in the Session Zero run with that spring's blunders and are refreshed each year; the binders live in the Day 1 box. The Day 1 teardown video is recorded by the instructor in one take before the session, in the pilot's prep week (the Phase 6 week-before window names the recording step, and the Session Zero Hour 3 kitting step confirms the saved file): Part 1, the 2023 case of the lawyer sanctioned after ChatGPT invented court citations; Part 2, Google AI Overviews' 2024 "glue on pizza" answer. Both are on the public record, and both are the reason this camp exists.

04 · A day at camp

The 60-minute lesson: one adult, twelve kids, zero dead time.

There is no 9-to-4 day and no AM/PM split. There is one 60-minute lesson of direct instruction, a 45-minute self-directed project block in the same room with the instructor present but not teaching, and 30 minutes of solo prep (8:45 to 9:15) before the doors open. Instruction is one hour a day; supervision is continuous, drop-off to pickup. The day runs 9:15 drop-off to 11:15 pickup, except Day 10, which runs to 11:45: the parents' showcase is pinned at 11:15-11:45 with the full cohort and the instructor present, and dismissal moves to 11:45. This is the whole schedule, and it is what makes one instructor enough.

8:45Instructor prepPull the day's box out of the storage closet and stage it at the instructor table, open it, lay out twelve stations, run the tool-safety check, top up shared devices. Pull the labeled table-supply caddy off the equipment shelf and set it on the side counter where the team tables can reach it: that is the morning motion that puts the 12 blunt-tip table scissors at the tables during lessons. The box stays at the instructor table all day: every "from the Day N box" step in that day's checklist pulls from the table, and no prep step sends the instructor back into the closet once campers are in the room. The checklist is printed; nothing is shopped for or improvised. The room is finished by 9:15: one adult cannot lay out twelve stations and run door check-in at the same time, so all 30 paid prep minutes land before drop-off, not after. Set one spare copy of the day's printed handouts on the door table, the late-arrival set. Re-plug the flatbed scanner's cable into the instructor laptop at the door table: the scanner stays on the table all session, but the laptop arrived with the instructor this morning and leaves at dismissal, so this is the morning step that performs the §08 equipment line's "each morning" re-plug, and the Day 1 scan-tab demo and the daily 11:15 pickup scans depend on it.
9:15Drop-offCampers check in at the door (QR + PIN against the Airtable roster), bags down, phones off or silent into the 12 name-labeled resealable phone pouches, seats in the U. A camper whose assigned buddy is absent that day is seated with the next present pair in board order, wrapping to the first present pair on the board, and that pair stands as the camper's accountability pair for the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form, so no field walk, dose gate, or incident form names a pair that does not exist that day. When two campers have absent buddies on the same day, each camper is counted from that camper's own pair position in board order, not from a previous substitute assignment, and a present pair that already hosts a substitute that day is skipped in the scan, so no present pair hosts two substitutes unless every present pair already hosts one, in which case the remaining camper joins the first present pair in board order. If no pair on the board is fully present that day, there is no present pair to scan to, so the orphans pair among themselves in board order, first orphan with second, third with fourth, and an odd orphan joins the first orphan pair as a trio, If only one camper is present at all, there is no second orphan to pair with, so that camper stays with the instructor as their accountability for the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form, and counts as the next unpaired orphan if a second camper arrives late, so the late camper pairs with them and the two form the accountability pair for the rest of the day. and those orphan pairs stand as the accountability pairs for the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form. The laminated board itself is not reprinted for a one-day substitution. On Day 1 only, the same door-table check-in collects the physical meds: a family whose enrollment Typeform names a medication hands the labeled med bag to the instructor, the instructor checks the bag against the med/allergy sheet in the instructor folder, and locks it in the med lockbox (EpiPens and rescue inhalers staged per the safety plan's field-bag rule), so no medication enters a camper bag on the first morning or any other. The door locks to visitors at 9:30. Late arrival: a camper arriving after the lock waits at the door with the parent, and the parent messages the camp number. The instructor opens the door from inside at the next minute-block boundary (9:40 or 10:10; after 10:10, at the next quarter-hour mark inside the project block, 10:45 or 11:00, since the instructor stays in the room through the project block, and no admission runs at 11:15 pickup itself, which is dismissal, not check-in), checks the camper in at the door table (QR + PIN against the Airtable roster), hands over the spare handout set, seats the camper with the buddy pair, and re-runs the count aloud with the new arrival. If the group is out on a field mission when the parent messages, no one is inside to open the door, and the instructor never leaves the group to admit: the instructor replies on the camp number with the group's expected return time, the parent and camper either wait at the door or take the camper home for the day, counted absent with no penalty, and a waiting camper is admitted at the first minute-block boundary after the group's return count, with the same door-table check-in, seating, and count re-run. A camper admitted during the project block joins the project corner with their accountability pair, and the pair walks them through the handout header at the table before either resumes work, which is that admission's catch-up, since the project block holds no minute-block transition for the buddy to carry it in. If the camper's assigned buddy is absent that day, the instructor seats the late camper with the next present pair in board order, wrapping to the first present pair on the board, and that pair carries the catch-up during the first transition and stands as the late camper's accountability pair for the day, so no seat, catch-up, or count depends on an absent child. When two campers have absent buddies on the same day, each camper is counted from that camper's own pair position in board order, not from a previous substitute assignment, and a present pair that already hosts a substitute that day is skipped in the scan, so no present pair hosts two substitutes unless every present pair already hosts one, in which case the remaining camper joins the first present pair in board order. If no pair on the board is fully present that day, there is no present pair to scan to, so the late camper pairs with the next unpaired orphan in board order, or joins the first orphan pair if every orphan is already paired, and that pair carries the catch-up during the first transition and stands as the late camper's accountability pair for the day. If the late camper is the only camper in the room at check-in, whether the day started with no camper present or the lone camper already departed before the late arrival, there is no buddy pair, no present pair, and no unpaired orphan to seat with, so that camper stays with the instructor as their accountability for the rest of the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form, and counts as the next unpaired orphan if another camper arrives later, so the later camper pairs with them and the two form the accountability pair for the rest of the day. The laminated board itself is not reprinted for a one-day substitution. The buddy carries the catch-up during the first transition. The instructor never leaves the room, and line of sight on the group never breaks.
9:30OpenToday's mission brief, crew role assignments read off the crew-rotation board (materials captain, Wall of Almost curator, reset lead). The board hangs beside the truth-rubric poster on the lab wall from Day 1 prep; the instructor reads the day's three names and points, no choosing, no negotiation. The rotation rule is fixed and printed on the board: alphabetical roster order, three new campers each day, each day's trio assigned in board order to materials captain, Wall of Almost curator, reset lead. Days 1-4 cover all twelve campers once; Days 5-8 cover everyone a second time; Days 9-10 take the first six alphabetically again, so every camper serves at least twice and no one serves twice before everyone has served once. The AI Fail of the Day opens Week 2 on Day 6 as a five-minute segment; on Day 1 it runs as a two-minute teaser, because the full teardown plays on the room screen in the Day 1 project block. It is not a daily segment, and the day-by-day run-of-shows below name it only on the two days that hold it.
9:40CoreOne activity; the instructor circulates for the full 30 minutes. Tool stations run only with the instructor standing at them.
10:10CloseDebrief, evidence logged, project-block handout issued, tomorrow previewed. The last five minutes (10:25-10:30) are the reset: handouts in backpacks, crew resets stations, campers move to the project corner in the same room, which is exactly what the 0:55-1:00 handoff segment of every day's run-of-show stages.
10:30-11:15Project block (self-directed)Campers work the printed handout in the project corner, in the same room. Blocks are paper-based, and every handout's header names its room-only steps: the room screen, the wall, the portfolio shelf, and a partner cannot ride home, so any step that uses them appears in the header's Room-only list and everything unnamed may finish at home. Day 6's listen-back needs the pair's recorder, Day 9's portfolio assembly and pair rehearsal need the room, and Day 10's polish and showcase need the room, so those three blocks never go home (no shared device ever leaves the room). The instructor stays in the room doing silent admin (attendance, incident log; on Day 9, the showcase program master, with the Day 10 box staged at the instructor table in that morning's prep). Zero instruction; continuous supervision.
11:15PickupCampers leave only with a person on the pickup-authorization list; photo-ID match at the door; Twilio pickup ping to parents, and in the same Twilio console moment the instructor cancels that day's pre-scheduled crisis broadcast, about one extra minute with the console already open: a day that ran normally sends nothing, and the broadcast fires only on a day the instructor never reaches the console, exactly the day it is needed. The 12 name-labeled resealable phone pouches come back at sign-out: each camper's phone comes out of its pouch at dismissal, the phone rides home in the backpack, and the empty pouch stays on the door table, so the next morning's 9:15 check-in finds all twelve where the drop-off row reaches for them, and the next session's twelve pouches never ride home in twelve backpacks. Early pickup: a parent collecting before 11:15 messages the camp number on arrival (or ahead of time through the Airtable preference); the instructor brings the camper to the door, matches photo ID against the pickup-authorization list, opens the door-table lockbox, pulls that camper's named pouch, hands the phone back, leaves the empty pouch on the door table, logs the sign-out in Airtable, and closes the door. The instructor stays in the room, and line of sight on the rest of the group never breaks. Late pickup: a camper not collected by 11:15 waits with the instructor at the door table, in the room, never off-site; after ten minutes the instructor calls the parent from the camp number, after twenty minutes the instructor calls the emergency contact from the Typeform intake, and no camper leaves the venue except with an authorized adult. A camper who wants a journal page in their QR-linked gallery flags it with a sticky-note scan-tab (the per-session pads on the door table) and hands the journal to the instructor at pickup; the instructor scans the flagged pages on the flatbed scanner between sign-outs, about 30 seconds a page, and the scans land in that camper's gallery folder on the laptop. The journal goes back in the backpack. On Day 10 this slot becomes the parents' showcase and dismissal moves to 11:45. After dismissal the instructor collects the 12 scissors off the team tables back into the table-supply caddy and recycles the unused late-arrival spare handout copy still on the door table (the spare the 8:45 prep staged and no late arrival claimed), then returns the caddy to the equipment shelf and relocks the day's box in the storage closet, the key staying with the instructor: the box is out of the closet only between the morning pull and the evening relock. On Day 10 only, the close beat stages the 12 pouches from the door table inside the combination-lock door-table lockbox, which the gallery strike's named-home teardown checklist packs into the Day 10 box, so the pouches ride the move-out with the box instead of riding home with the campers. When a W-2 instructor teaches, dismissal closes with the end-of-day folder handoff: the instructor pulls the instructor folder out of the field bag at the door table and hands it to the founder, who carries it home for the evening transcription. Every evening step that needs the folder, the pre-transcription Airtable check, the transcription itself, the founder's-checklist margin notes, and the marked-sheet retirement to the trip folder, runs off the folder in founder hands that evening. Before the folder leaves the instructor's hands, the instructor fills the day's end-of-day summary digest, about five minutes at the door table after the sign-out count hits zero, on the one-page digest template: date, day number, and instructor initials; attendance (present count, late arrivals and early pickups with full name and time, matching the pickup-authorization list, since the digest never leaves the instructor and the founder); incidents (tier and one line each, pointing at the full forms already filed in the folder, never a second record); meds administered (count, read off the log sheet); capstone progress flags (any camper blocked or off-track, by name); materials low or broken (what to re-order before tomorrow); parent messages pending (camp-number voicemail or texts not yet returned); tomorrow's needs (one line). The folder handoff is the digest's delivery: the founder reads it off the folder at the evening transcription, beside the incident rows. When the founder teaches, the director is the instructor, so the same five minutes fills the same page and files it in the folder as the session's daily record, no send needed. The five minutes sit inside the existing post-dismissal close beat, after the count and before the folder leaves, so the paid-day itemization does not move. At the next teaching morning's 8:45 prep the founder returns the folder to the instructor at the door table before the day's box leaves the storage closet, and the instructor files it back in the field bag before check-in opens, so the morning checklist's deferred-transcription read-off opens off the same folder the evening worked from, and the read-off opens as a joint beat: the instructor reads it aloud off the printed morning checklist while the founder, on site at 8:45 returning the folder, runs the check-plus-transcription on the spot before the checklist closes (when the founder teaches, the founder reads the read-off and runs the check-plus-transcription as the single on-site adult). When the founder will be absent the next teaching morning, the handoff is skipped under the Day 3 founder-absent morning rule: the instructor keeps the folder overnight, and this pass names the instructor-arrival folder move beside it: on the next teaching morning's 8:45 prep the instructor pulls the instructor folder out of the relocked field bag at the door table and sets the folder on the door table before the day's box leaves the storage closet, then files it back in the field bag before check-in opens, mirroring the folder-arrival handoff beat, so the solo read-off opens off the same folder and the morning's margin logging, knock-note teardown notes, and 11:15 pickup-row reads all file into the same folder at the same door table on a morning with no founder on site; the move fires only when the folder is in instructor custody overnight, never when the folder stayed at the founder's home after the before-8:30 call, never on a no-notice morning, and never after check-in opens, so the folder is in place before the first camper arrives; on the founder's first on-site return after a founder-absent morning, the return-morning folder handoff runs at 8:45 prep at the door table before the joint read-off opens: the instructor hands the folder to the founder, the folder moving from the door table into the returning founder's hands, logged as 'return-morning folder handoff, ' with time and the instructor's initials beside the words in the morning checklist margin at the door table, so the joint read-off opens only off a logged handoff, because the joint beat requires the folder in founder hands. The handoff fires only when the folder is in instructor custody overnight after a founder-absent morning, never when the folder stayed at the founder's home, where the folder-arrival handoff governs instead, and never after check-in opens; it is distinct from the return-before-pickup re-claim, which runs mid-day on a founder-arrival morning where the folder never left the door table, and from the mid-lesson emergency-departure return, which is a re-claim, not a handoff. Beside the return-morning folder handoff this pass pins the mid-lesson return handoff: on a founder-absent morning, when the founder returns on site mid-lesson after the instructor-arrival move already placed the folder at the door table, the folder waits at the door table; the instructor never leaves the room mid-lesson, so no handoff fires mid-lesson and the return is never treated as a re-claim, because the re-claims cover only the folder-never-left-the-door-table cases on founder-arrival mornings. At the next block transition the instructor hands the folder to the founder at the door table, the folder moving from the door table into the founder's hands, logged as 'return-morning folder handoff, ' with time and the instructor's initials beside the words in the morning checklist margin at the door table, marked 'deferred from 8:45, mid-lesson return' beside the line, so the joint read-off's folder condition is restored by a logged handoff, not by a silent re-take. A return in the final block before 11:15 has no later transition, so the handoff runs at the 11:15 pickup-row instead, the same log line marked the same way; the mid-lesson return never restructures the lesson and never takes the room, the lesson holding under the named teacher until dismissal. Beside the mid-lesson return handoff this pass pins the mid-lesson founder waiting place: on a mid-lesson return the founder waits at the door-table or foyer area outside the lesson room, silent and still, until the next block transition; the founder does not enter the room mid-lesson and does not sit among the camper desks, because a founder in the room mid-lesson would pull campers' attention and undercut the lesson-hold the pin protects. At a venue with no separate door-table or foyer space the founder waits off-venue, in the car or the building lobby, until the next block transition, and a return in the final block keeps the same waiting place until the 11:15 pickup-row runs the handoff. The waiting-place pin fires only in instructor-custody-after-founder-absent mornings, never on founder-arrival mornings, and never touches the mid-lesson emergency-departure return, which is a re-claim, not a handoff. Beside the mid-lesson founder waiting place this pass pins the mid-lesson handoff-ready signal: on a mid-lesson return with the founder waiting off-venue, the founder self-times the posted transition minute on their own watch against the standing posted day grid, 9:30 open, 9:40 core, 10:10 close, 10:30 project block, 11:15 pickup, and walks in to wait, silent and still, at the door-table threshold at that minute; the signal is the founder's own watch-check, and nobody signals mid-lesson. If the instructor has not yet reached the door table at the posted minute, the founder holds at the threshold until the instructor's transition move runs the handoff, and the log line records the actual time, still marked 'deferred from 8:45, mid-lesson return'. If the founder is not at the threshold when the transition fires, the handoff defers to the next posted minute on the grid, and a return in the final block defers to the 11:15 pickup-row instead, under the mid-lesson return handoff's final-block rule. The handoff-ready signal pin fires only in instructor-custody-after-founder-absent mornings, never on founder-arrival mornings, and never touches the mid-lesson emergency-departure return, which is a re-claim, not a handoff. Beside the handoff-ready signal this pass pins the official camp time source, the pin the last pass described but never placed in the flow body: the room clock is the authority for every posted minute on the day grid and every minute in the minute-by-minute run-of-show. At 8:45 prep, before the day's box leaves the storage closet, the instructor stands at the room clock, checks it against a network-time reference, the phone's automatic time, moves the hands to match, and logs 'clock synced ' with the date and initials in the morning checklist margin at the door table, the check riding the 8:45 prep the plan already staffs. In a room with no clock, the instructor's phone, on automatic time, sits face-up at the door table as the day's official time source, never leaving the door table until the last camper is collected. The lesson beats read off the room clock, never off a wristwatch, and the founder's threshold self-timing reads the room clock's minute at the threshold, so a watch running minutes off misfires nothing: the threshold hold starts on the room's minute. The pin fires every teaching day and never re-runs mid-lesson, because the instructor never leaves the room to adjust the clock. Beside the time source this pass pins the midday clock-failure rule: a battery dying mid-lesson, the hands freezing at 9:41, or the door-table phone fallback's screen sleeping would kill the authority silently, so the rule names the pinned observation moment. At every posted transition, when the instructor reaches the door table for the transition move the plan already staffs, the instructor glances at the room clock and checks its minute against the phone's automatic time. If the minute disagrees by more than two, or the hands have not moved since the last transition, the authority switches to the instructor's phone on automatic time, placed face-up on the door table for the rest of the day, and the instructor logs 'clock failed , phone authority' with the date and initials in the morning checklist margin at that transition. The clock is never touched or re-synced mid-lesson, because the instructor never leaves the room. When the phone is the authority, the founder's threshold self-timing reads the phone's minute at the threshold instead of the clock's. At the day's close the founder swaps the clock battery, drawing the fresh AA from the labeled clock-battery envelope on the door table beside the scanner, or restores the phone fallback, logged in the same margin, so the next day opens on a synced authority again. The rule fires only when the authority dies midday, never on a clean day, and never touches the 8:45 sync. The same row pins the founder-facing notice of a midday authority switch beside the clock-failure rule: at the same transition move where the authority switches, the instructor folds one sheet of the camp's paper stock into a standing tent card, writes 'OFFICIAL TIME: PHONE, NOT WALL CLOCK' with the door-table marker, and turns it face-out at the door table; a founder arriving at a posted minute out of habit reads the card at the threshold and reads the instructor's phone face-up at the door table for the minute instead of the dead wall clock, so the threshold self-timing misfires nothing; the card is never moved or touched mid-lesson, because the instructor never leaves the room, and at the day's close the founder retires the card with the clock battery swap, logged in the same margin, so the next day opens with no stale notice on the door table; the notice rides the transition move the plan already staffs, fires only when the authority switches, and never touches a clean day. The same row pins the threshold-hold authority-switch notice beside the founder-facing notice: the waiting-place pin has the mid-lesson returning founder holding silent and still at the threshold while the instructor runs the transition move, and the switch observation happens inside that same move, so a card turned face-out could sit unread if the held founder's eyes never find it; the instructor turns the card face-out where the held founder can read it from the threshold, catches the founder's eye, and taps the card once with a finger, no words, no wave; the held founder reads the card and reads the instructor's phone face-up at the door table for the minute instead of the dead wall clock for any remaining threshold self-timing, holding silent and still otherwise; the tap is a direct reading cue inside the staffed transition move, never a signal sent mid-lesson, so the nobody-signals-mid-lesson pin and the lesson-hold pin are untouched. Missed-tap confirmation: the instructor waits after the tap for the held founder's single nod, the pinned read-back, before the transition move ends. If no nod lands, the founder looking away with eyes on their own phone when the tap landed, the instructor re-cues once with a second, firmer tap on the card inside the same transition move, still a direct reading cue to a present founder, never a signal sent mid-lesson. If the founder still gives no read-back, the instructor treats the notice as unconfirmed, logs 'authority switch unconfirmed, no founder read-back ' with the date and initials in the morning checklist margin at that transition, and leaves the card face-out at the threshold's outer edge where the held founder's next downward glance lands on it; the unconfirmed branch fires only after the re-tap, never on a first tap, and the card is retired with the battery swap at the day's close either way. The stale-line day-close rule fires with that same close: if an unconfirmed line naming a founder is still open when the card is retired, because the named founder made no second threshold arrival that day, the instructor draws a single line through the open line in the morning checklist margin, writes 'void at day close, no second arrival ' beside it, and the line never rolls forward; the next day's fresh morning checklist opens with no open lines and no standing notice, and a fresh next-day authority switch re-fires the founder-facing notice from that day's own rule rather than inheriting the voided line. The stale-line day-close rule fires on teaching days only: a final-day-of-session open line is voided by the move-out's own beat, not by this rule. The move-out void beat fires before the numbered sheets retire at the move-out close: the instructor draws a single line through any still-open unconfirmed line in the final day's morning checklist margin, writes 'void at move-out, no second arrival ' beside it, and the line files with the retired final-day checklist, legible as voided; the line never rolls forward and the next session opens on fresh sheets, so no stale notice survives the session. The final-day card retirement beat fires at the move-out close for a still-face-out authority-switch notice card: before the numbered sheets retire, the instructor turns the card face-in at the door table, logs 'card retired at move-out ' in the morning checklist margin, and files the card flat into the retired-card pocket; the pocket envelope files with the retired final-day checklist; the beat fires on the session's final teaching day only and only while the card still stands face-out, never when the lucky-agree close already turned the card face-in that day, never when no card ever stood, and never on ordinary teaching days, where the ordinary-day battery-swap retirement keeps its own beat; the beat rides the staffed move-out sheet retire with campers present, the instructor never leaves the room, and no mid-lesson signal is sent. The face-in card move-out collection beat covers a card the lucky-agree close turned face-in mid-day on the session's final teaching day: during the staffed move-out sheet retire, the instructor collects the face-in card from the door table, logs 'face-in card collected at move-out , lucky-agree close mid-day' in the morning checklist margin, and files the card flat into the retired-card pocket beside the still-face-out card's retirement note; the card files in the pocket, it never returns to the paper stock box, so a used, creased card cannot re-enter as fresh stock; the pocket envelope files with the retired final-day checklist. The beat fires on the session's final teaching day only, only when a card was turned face-in by the lucky-agree close that day, and never on ordinary teaching days, where the ordinary-day battery-swap retirement keeps its own beat; the collection rides the staffed move-out sheet retire with campers present, the instructor never leaves the room, and no mid-lesson signal is sent. The ordinary-day face-in card collection beat covers a card the lucky-agree close turned face-in mid-day on an ordinary teaching day: during the staffed ordinary-day close, beside the battery-swap retirement, which takes down a still-face-out card and files it into the retired-card pocket, the instructor collects the face-in card from the door table, logs 'face-in card collected at day close , lucky-agree close mid-day' in the morning checklist margin, and files the card flat into the retired-card pocket beside the battery-swap retirement note; the card files in the pocket, it never returns to the paper stock box, so a used, creased card cannot re-enter as fresh stock. The retired-card pocket is a kraft envelope from the retired-card-pocket stock, labeled with the date, pulled from that day's box beside the morning checklist at the morning prep and clipped behind the morning checklist on the instructor table, holding every retired card that day; at the close the envelope files into the day's box before the box relocks in the closet. Before the envelope files at the close, the instructor re-verifies the label: the instructor reads the envelope's date label aloud against the date on the morning checklist's margin lines, initials the match in the margin, and only then files the envelope, into the day's box on ordinary days, with the retired final-day checklist on the session's final teaching day. If the margin carries no dated line yet that day, the instructor writes the date once as the margin anchor and initials it first. The written date is pinned to the instructor phone's automatic date, the same phone the dead-clock beat names as the session's time authority: before any anchor is written or any label matched, the instructor opens the phone's automatic date display and reads the date once, and every date written that day comes from the phone's date, never from memory; the envelope's date label is read aloud against the phone-checked margin date and the match initialed. On a mismatch with the phone's automatic date, the phone's date wins and both written dates are corrected to it before the envelope files, so an instructor-written date typo cannot pass both checks. If the instructor phone is dead at the close, the instructor falls back to the margin-line read alone, logs 'phone dead, date not cross-checked' with initials in the margin, and charges the phone before the next morning's prep. On a margin-to-envelope mismatch, the instructor re-labels the envelope with the margin's date before it files, so a mislabeled or stale envelope never files as the wrong day's record and the pocket's cards never detach from their margin lines. The re-verification rides the staffed close with campers present, fires on every teaching day, and never sends a mid-lesson signal. The beat fires on ordinary teaching days only, only when a card was turned face-in by the lucky-agree close that day, never when no card ever stood, and never on the session's final teaching day, where the move-out collection keeps its own beat; the collection rides the staffed ordinary-day close with campers present, the instructor never leaves the room, and no mid-lesson signal is sent. The off-minute reconcile beat fires at any later same-day founder threshold arrival: at the pickup flow's threshold ID beat, the instructor checks the morning checklist margin for an open unconfirmed line naming the arriving founder; if one is open, the instructor reads the minute on the founder's wristwatch at the threshold and checks it against the phone authority on the door table; if the founder's minute disagrees with the phone authority by more than two, the instructor shows the phone face-up at the door table, taps the card once where it stands face-out at the threshold's outer edge, waits for the single nod read-back, and logs 'unconfirmed notice reconciled ' in the margin at that arrival, the instructor's initials beside the words, closing the open line; the beat fires only for a founder with an open unconfirmed line and only when the minutes disagree, never on clean days, never on confirmed switches, never when the minutes agree; the beat rides the threshold ID beat the pickup flow already staffs, the instructor never leaves the room, and line of sight holds. The lucky-agree arrival close fires at any later same-day founder threshold arrival where the reconcile beat's margin check finds an open unconfirmed line naming the arriving founder and the founder's wristwatch minute agrees with the phone authority on the door table: the instructor taps the card once where it stands face-out at the threshold's outer edge, waits for the single nod read-back that the founder reads the card and reads the phone's minute instead of the dead wall clock for any remaining threshold self-timing, and logs 'unconfirmed notice reconciled ' in the margin at that arrival, the instructor's initials beside the words, closing the open line; the close fires only for a founder with an open unconfirmed line and only when the minutes agree, never on clean days, never on confirmed switches, never when no open line names the arriving founder, and once every open line in the margin is closed the instructor turns the card face-in at the door table at that same beat, retiring the standing notice, since no unconfirmed notice remains to display; a second same-day midday arrival by the same founder then runs the normal threshold ID beat with no notice handling, the line already closed and the card already retired; the close rides the threshold ID beat the pickup flow already staffs, the instructor never leaves the room, and line of sight holds. The deferred handoff fires only in instructor-custody-after-founder-absent mornings; and in an emergency the founder calls or texts before 8:30 and the folder stays at the founder's home; on a no-notice morning, no call or text before 8:30 and no founder at 8:45 prep, the instructor opens 8:45 prep on a missing folder and a missing founder under the Day 3 no-notice rule, teaching from the day's box alone and logging 'founder absent, no contact received, 8:45' with the instructor's initials beside the words and the date in the morning checklist margin at the door table. A dead instructor phone moves the late-call check to the venue office's desk phone under the Day 3 dead-instructor-phone rule, the instructor logging 'instructor phone dead, no call could have been received, venue-office callback attempted, 8:45' with the instructor's initials beside the words and the date in the same margin; when the venue office is locked and unstaffed, the same rule's locked-venue-office branch opens the no-notice solo read unchanged and logs 'venue office locked, desk-phone callback unavailable, no contact received, 8:45', with the instructor's initials beside the words and the date in the same margin, and the office re-tried before 9:15 drop-off. The re-try's "if staff arrives" condition is unobservable to one adult at the door table, so the branch pins the knock-note beside the locked-venue-office branch: at the 8:45 one-knock moment the instructor tapes a knock-note to the office door, written on the camp's paper stock with the door-table marker, reading 'Ground Truth Summer Camp, main room. Instructor phone dead, one adult with campers until 9:15 drop-off. Need the office desk phone for a 2-minute call before 9:15. Please knock on the main room door when the office opens, or leave this door open.' When staff knock on the main room door or leave the office door open during prep, the instructor walks to the office while no campers are in the room, asks for the founder's number from the venue's event file, and runs the dead-phone desk-phone call the rule would have run at 8:45, logging 'desk-phone callback made , ' with the date beside the 8:45 locked log in the same margin, and the instructor's initials beside the words in the same margin, so the contact-attempt record is amended the moment the backup path becomes usable. The signal window closes at 9:15 drop-off: once campers are in the room the instructor no longer leaves it, and the unamended 8:45 locked log stands as the day's contact-attempt record. The knock-note is pinned only on the dead-instructor-phone no-notice morning's locked-office branch; a staffed office at 8:45 runs the desk-phone call directly with no note, and no other morning tapes anything to the office door. The note comes down the same day it went up, pinned here as the knock-note teardown: after the last camper is collected at the 11:15 pickup (11:45 on the Day 10 showcase), while no campers are in the room, the instructor walks to the office door, removes the knock-note, writes 'retired ' across it, initials it with the instructor's initials, the hand that ran the teardown, and files it in the instructor folder beside the morning checklist margin page carrying the 8:45 locked log, so the retired note sits next to the day's contact-attempt record. The in every 'retired ' mark on the page takes the spelled-out month and day, written 'October 2', never digits like '10/2', so no retired note reads an ambiguous date as a fact: the same convention covers the shift-handoff note's 'retired , callback ' mark and the mid-lesson departure's 'retired , founder emergency departure mid-lesson' marking. A morning that taped nothing to the office door runs no teardown: only the dead-phone no-notice morning's locked-office branch owns the knock-note teardown, so a stale note can never ask staff to knock for a phone that is no longer dead. A founder whose pre-8:30 call or text to the instructor's phone goes unanswered drives to the venue under the Day 3 founder-no-answer rule and arrives in person before 9:15 with the folder, the arrival re-entering the day under the first-contact rule. On a founder-arrival morning the knock-note teardown owner is the folder-holding founder, not the instructor who taped the note: after the last camper is collected at the 11:15 pickup (11:45 on the Day 10 showcase), while no campers are in the room, the founder walks to the office door, removes the knock-note, writes 'retired ' across it, initials it with the founder's initials, the hand that ran the teardown, not the instructor's (the folder's owner), and files it in the instructor folder at the door table beside the morning checklist margin page carrying the 8:45 locked log, so the retired note sits next to the day's contact-attempt record; the instructor never re-runs the teardown and never delegates it, and the founder never hands it back, so two adults on site can neither both do it nor each assume the other did. On a founder-arrival morning the founder stays on site through the 11:15 pickup, because the founder owns the knock-note teardown: the founder's day on site ends only after the teardown is filed in the instructor folder. A founder who must depart before pickup re-assigns the teardown to the instructor in person at the door table at a block transition, between the 60-minute lesson and the 45-minute project block, never mid-lesson, and the instructor logs 'knock-note teardown re-assigned to instructor at , founder departing' with the date in the morning checklist margin at the door table beside the teardown-owner pin, so the 11:15 moment always has a named owner on paper: the founder on site owns it, or the margin log names the instructor. The on the re-assignment margin log takes digits plus AM/PM, written '10:42 AM', never bare digits like '10:42', so a midday handoff never reads as an afternoon one on the paper trail. The re-assignment margin log line carries the instructor's initials beside the words, the same convention as the cap log line, so the paper trail attributes the handoff to the instructor's hand. The on the re-assignment margin log takes the spelled-out month and day, written 'October 2', never digits like '10/2', so the paper trail never reads an ambiguous date as a fact, the same date convention the mid-lesson departure line carries. A departure with no logged re-assignment is not a departure under this rule, so there is no silent departure; the founder stays through pickup. A real incapacitation never runs the dead-phone rule, so this pin never enters the Tier 3 path. On a founder-arrival morning, a founder emergency that cannot wait for a block transition overrides the never-mid-lesson rule: the lesson holds. The founder is the backup adult on this morning, never the teacher; the instructor was always the lesson's sole named teacher, so the founder's mid-lesson departure neither suspends nor restructures the 60-minute lesson, and the emergency departure never pulls the instructor from the room, because no camper is ever left without the named teacher. The founder tells the instructor in person at the room door if any seconds allow it ('leaving now, emergency'); if no seconds allow it, the instructor logs 'founder emergency departure mid-lesson at , no re-assignment possible' with the date in the morning checklist margin at the door table at the next block transition, beside the teardown-owner pin. The date takes the spelled-out month and day, written 'October 2', never digits like '10/2', so the paper trail never reads an ambiguous date as a fact. The on that same departure line takes digits plus AM/PM, written '10:42 AM', never bare digits like '10:42', so a morning departure never reads as an afternoon one on the paper trail. The departure line carries the instructor's initials beside the words, the same convention as the cap log line, so the paper trail attributes the departure to the instructor's hand. Teardown ownership then defaults to the instructor: after the last camper is collected at the 11:15 pickup (11:45 on the Day 10 showcase), while no campers are in the room, the instructor walks to the office door, removes the knock-note, writes 'retired , founder emergency departure mid-lesson' across it, initials it with the instructor's initials, the hand the departure default named, the same convention as the cap log line, and files it in the instructor folder at the door table beside the morning checklist margin page carrying the 8:45 locked log and the departure line, so the 11:15 moment has a named owner on paper even with no re-assignment possible. A real incapacitation of the founder on site never runs the dead-phone rule either and stays Tier 3, 911 first, so this pin covers only the founder leaving under their own power; the founder-no-answer drive rule and the first-contact rule are untouched. The instructor folder never leaves the door table on a teaching day. On a founder-arrival morning the founder arrived with the folder, but custody moves to the door table at arrival and does not move again until dismissal's end-of-day folder handoff, so the mid-lesson emergency departure never carries the folder off site: the departing founder leaves the folder on the door table, because the instructor logs the departure line into the folder's morning checklist margin at the next block transition beside the teardown-owner pin, and the teardown the defaulted instructor owns after the 11:15 pickup files into that same folder, so a folder taken off site would take the day's log and the filing target with it. A founder who returns on site before the 11:15 pickup re-claims under the return-before-pickup rule exactly as pinned: at the in-person door-table re-claim the instructor hands the folder back to the returning founder, restoring the folder-holding founder the re-claim names, and the re-claim log runs as the pin already says, because the folder never left the door table in between, so there is no off-site folder to chase and no absent filing target. A mid-lesson emergency departure is never a handoff: the folder's only two moves on a founder-arrival morning are the arrival move to the door table and the dismissal's end-of-day handoff back to the founder. The first of the two moves is pinned by name here as the folder-arrival handoff: at 8:45 prep the founder returns the folder to the instructor at the door table before the day's box leaves the storage closet, the instructor files it in the field bag before check-in opens, and the founder does not carry the folder past the door table into the lesson, so the folder a mid-lesson emergency departure leaves on the door table is the same folder the arrival handoff placed, never a folder the founder carried into the room. On a founder-arrival morning where the founder emergency-departed mid-lesson and teardown ownership defaulted to the instructor with the logged departure line, a founder who returns on site before the 11:15 pickup does not silently re-take the knock-note teardown. The logged default stays with the instructor unless the returning founder re-claims it in person at the door table before the last camper is collected: the instructor logs 'knock-note teardown re-claimed by returning founder at ' with the date in the morning checklist margin at the door table beside the departure line, and the re-claim names the folder-holding founder, because the teardown files into the instructor folder. A founder who returns after the teardown is filed changes nothing: no re-do, no re-log, the filed note stands. A founder who returns during the lesson neither restructures it nor takes the room: the lesson holds under the named teacher the way the emergency-departure pin already holds it, and the return itself is logged at the next block transition. Every return is logged at the door table: the instructor writes 'founder returned on site at ' with the date in the morning checklist margin beside the departure line, then the ownership state after it, re-claim logged and named or default held, so the 11:15 moment always has a named owner on paper. On an instructor-only morning the instructor owns the teardown exactly as the pin above names it. The teardown owner cannot drift into the Tier 3 real-incapacitation path: a real incapacitation never runs the dead-phone rule, so no note is ever taped. On a dead-phone morning the 11:15 pickup-row Twilio console moment moves to the instructor laptop under the Day 3 dead-phone Twilio path: the laptop is the instructor's own machine, it sits at the door table all session with the flatbed scanner plugged into it, and the venue wi-fi the §08 card sizes for 8+ laptops reaches it, so the instructor opens the Twilio console in the laptop browser on the instructor's own restricted login, fires the pickup pings, and cancels that day's pre-scheduled crisis broadcast in the same console moment, about one extra minute with the console already open. The eight cart laptops are camper devices and carry no instructor login, and the venue office's desk area names no browser, the desk phone being voice-only, so the instructor laptop is the only on-site console device on a dead-phone morning. If the laptop cannot reach the console, the instructor logs 'Twilio console unreachable at 11:15, pings skipped, broadcast not cancelled' with the date in the morning checklist margin at the door table, pickup runs on the in-person door-table flow unchanged, and the 11:16 broadcast fires as the dead-man's switch is built to fire; the instructor tells each arriving parent at the door that the 'instructor incapacitated' text is the switch firing on a dead phone, not an emergency. The false fire still reaches the founder's cell and the break-glass adult's cell, so the Day 3 false-fired stand-down rule runs beside the console-unreachable branch: before 12:00 that day the instructor accounts the false fire to the founder, in person at the dismissal folder handoff when the founder is present, or, when the founder is absent that day, by a call to the founder's cell from the venue office desk phone before 12:00; the account names the 11:16 broadcast firing on a dead phone (11:46 on the Day 10 showcase), the day running normally, and the parents told at the door. The founder writes '11:16 broadcast false-fired on dead instructor phone, day ran normally, parents told at the door, instructor confirmed ' with the date in the founder's own checklist margin, and the logged account stands down the sick-day bullet's 4 PM founder run and the 9 PM break-glass trigger for that day, because both fire only on an unconfirmed broadcast and the logged account is the confirmation; instead of the 4 PM run the founder sends a one-line correction text over the camp number to every session parent from the owner login in the Twilio console: 'Correction: the 11:16 text was the camp safety broadcast firing on a dead phone, not an emergency. The day ran normally and all campers are dismissed.' When the founder is the instructor that day, the false fire is self-evident: the founder wrote the margin line, so the founder sends the correction text and the triggers never arm. If the instructor cannot reach the founder by noon, because the office is locked and the founder is absent with no handoff, the instructor logs 'founder not reached by noon, false-fire account unaccounted' with the date in the same margin, and the 4 PM run still fires on the unconfirmed broadcast: before running the checklist the founder calls the venue office on the T-14 roster's designated contact line and asks whether the day's session ran and campers were dismissed; a venue confirmation of a normal day stands the run down, the founder logging it as a venue-confirmed false-fire account and sending the correction text; a closed office or no confirmation lets the 4 PM run proceed on the unconfirmed premise, because a real incapacitation must not wait on a second confirmation channel. The false-fired broadcast carries one more exposure the stand-down does not cover: a parent who missed the door explanation and the correction text can call 911 on the false 'instructor incapacitated, 911 called' text, and dispatch can send responding units to the venue, so the Day 3 false-alarm reception script runs beside the stand-down rule: the instructor never leaves the room, one adult and twelve campers, and meets responding officers at the room door with the group in sight, the campers counted and seated; the instructor shows the door-table margin log, the '11:16 broadcast false-fired on dead instructor phone, day ran normally, parents told at the door, instructor confirmed ' line with the date, and the correction-text record, the one-line correction text sent to every session parent from the owner login in the Twilio console, the console log on the instructor laptop being the written false-fire record; when the venue office called the camp on a responding-unit arrival the instructor takes the call at the room door and the venue's designated emergency contact, named in the launch plan, escorts units from the entrance, the instructor's line of sight on the group never breaking; after the stand-down the instructor logs 'responding officers arrived on false 11:16 text, margin log and correction text shown, stood down, day continues' with the date in the door-table margin. The false-fire chain ends on the dispatch side, not at the room door: after the correction text fires, the founder calls dispatch's non-emergency line from the founder's own cell, never the camp number, which still routes to the dead instructor phone. The number is captured at the T-2 site visit and written into the launch plan beside the route card's 911 location line, so a future founder is not searching for it mid-incident. The founder reports the false text: the camp's name, the venue address off the launch plan, the 11:16 broadcast time (11:46 on the Day 10 showcase), the account the instructor confirmed before noon (the dead-man's text fired on a dead instructor phone, the day ran normally, a correction text went to every session parent from the owner login, responding officers stood down on scene or no units had arrived yet), and asks dispatch to cancel any pending response to the original 911 text, taking the operator's name and the disposition, cancelled, still rolling, or already on scene. The founder logs 'dispatch non-emergency called , operator , false 11:16 text reported, response ' with the date in the founder's own checklist margin, then accounts the disposition to the instructor the way the stand-down account travels, in person at dismissal or by the venue office desk phone, and the instructor logs 'founder dispatch cancel: non-emergency called , operator , units , founder confirmed ' with the date in the door-table margin, so the door log carries the dispatch call's outcome beside the reception log. The non-emergency line does not always answer, so the Day 3 non-emergency queue fallback runs beside the dispatch-side cancel. When the line puts the founder on a hold queue, the founder stays on the queue, because the cancel is the difference between units rolling and units standing down and the reception script runs regardless, so no teaching minute is spent waiting; a queue that drops the call is re-dialed once, and a second drop is treated as the voicemail branch. When the line goes to voicemail, the founder leaves one message: the camp's name, the venue address off the launch plan, the 11:16 false broadcast time (11:46 on the Day 10 showcase), the false-fire account (the dead-man's text fired on a dead instructor phone, the day ran normally, a correction text went to every session parent from the owner login, responding officers stood down on scene or no units had arrived yet), a request to cancel any pending response to the original 911 text and to log that the 911 call named in the text never happened, and a callback number, the founder's own cell, never the camp number, which still routes to the dead instructor phone, plus the session's calendar date so dispatch can match the report to the day. The founder logs 'dispatch non-emergency voicemail left , callback , false 11:16 text reported, no operator confirmation' with the date in the founder's own checklist margin, then accounts the voicemail outcome to the instructor the way the stand-down account travels, in person at dismissal or by the venue office desk phone, and the instructor logs 'founder dispatch voicemail fallback: non-emergency voicemail left , callback , founder confirmed ' with the date in the door-table margin beside the reception log. If dispatch never confirms the cancel by the close of the teaching day, the instructor logs 'dispatch cancel unconfirmed at day close, voicemail callback pending, day ran normally' with the date in the door-table margin, so the unconfirmed state is on paper and the reception script's door meeting stands as the day's last word on the false alarm. The queue fallback inherits the cancel's firing rule: only on a logged false fire, only after the correction text has gone out, never on an unconfirmed broadcast. The reception script runs regardless of the dispatch call's outcome: cancelled responses and still-rolling units meet the same door meeting, the margin log shown first. The dispatch-side cancel fires only on a logged false fire, and only after the correction text has gone out; a real incapacitation still runs Tier 3, 911 first, and no dispatch line is ever cancelled on an unconfirmed broadcast. When the founder is the instructor on a false-fire day, the founder's own cell is the dead instructor phone, so the dispatch-side cancel cannot run from it: after the correction text fires, the founder runs the cancel from the venue office desk phone once the day's dismissal is complete, with the room empty and the campers picked up, the same desk phone the stand-down account already travels on, never the camp number that still routes to the dead phone. The founder gives the same report, takes the same operator name and disposition, and logs 'dispatch non-emergency called , operator , false 11:16 text reported, response ' with the date in the door-table margin, because on this day the founder is the instructor and the door-table margin is the founder's log, so there is no founder's-own-checklist write and no handoff: the folder never leaves founder hands. The queue fallback rides the same after-dismissal desk-phone moment: a hold queue is stayed on from the desk phone, a dropped queue is re-dialed once then treated as the voicemail branch, and the voicemail message is the same message except the callback number is the venue office desk phone's number, never the founder's dead cell, with the founder re-checking the desk phone for a dispatch callback on the next teaching morning's 8:45 arrival and logging the outcome in the door-table margin beside the reception log. The venue-staff callback briefing rides the same after-dismissal desk-phone moment: before leaving the office, the founder briefs the venue's designated emergency contact, named in the launch plan, and the staff member on the office desk, in person, that dispatch may call the desk phone back overnight on the number the voicemail left. The briefing names the account the staff give on the callback, the false-fired 11:16 text for the camp on the session's calendar date, no emergency, the instructor on site, the stand-down already run; the contact path on this day, because the founder's own cell is dead: the staff write the operator name, the time of the call, and the message on the camp's event-file page at the venue office, and the founder reads that page at the next teaching morning's 8:45 arrival desk-phone re-check and transcribes any callback into the door-table margin beside the reception log, so the briefing, the write location, and the re-check form one chain; and the rule that a message about the already-stood-down false alarm is never re-escalated, no second 911 call over a dispatch callback. The note covers the overnight shift the briefing cannot reach: at the same after-dismissal desk-phone moment, the founder writes a shift-handoff note on the camp's paper stock and tapes it to the office desk phone's handset cradle, so whoever answers that phone next sees it before lifting the handset. The note carries the account the staff give on the callback, the camp name, the false-fired 11:16 text for the camp on the session's calendar date, no emergency, the instructor on site, the stand-down already run; the write location, the camp's event-file page at the venue office, with the operator name, the time of the call, and the message written there; the never-re-escalate rule, no second 911 call over a message about the already-stood-down false alarm; and the founder's 8:45 arrival re-check, when any overnight callback gets transcribed into the door-table margin beside the reception log. The briefing and the note are pinned for this day only: on a non-founder-as-instructor false-fire day the voicemail leaves the founder's own working cell as the callback number, so no venue-staff briefing and no shift-handoff note are pinned there.The day-close unconfirmed log is written in the door-table margin on this day too. The cancel still fires only on a logged false fire, and only after the correction text has gone out; a real incapacitation still runs Tier 3, 911 first, and no dispatch line is ever cancelled on an unconfirmed broadcast. When the founder teaches, there is no handoff: the founder is the instructor and the folder never leaves founder hands.
11:15-11:45Parents' showcase (Day 10 only)Twelve stations, the Wall of Almost, the failed-print museum. The whole cohort stays in the room with the instructor; parents tour and watch each camper's 60-second pitch, run off the station script in the Day 10 handoff: fresh arrivals get the intro plus the pitch, late arrivals get the three-sentence version, and no parent gets all three. The instructor counts all twelve aloud before guests enter, and no camper leaves until the 11:45 authorized-adult dismissal with photo-ID match. At the door, each camper hands the camp pen back to the instructor as they sign out, before the journal rides home in the backpack, so the strike's pack-back beat works off twelve pens that stayed in the room, not off journals that have already left it. At the same sign-out the instructor opens the med lockbox and the lockable medication cooler bag and hands each camper's medication (EpiPens, rescue inhalers, refrigerated meds) to the authorized adult named on the med/allergy sheet, logged in the Airtable med log with time and the instructor's initials, so no camper prescription medication stays at the venue between sessions, the same rule the mid-session medication swap already enforces when a prescription changes (the old medication is handed back the same day, never left at the venue overnight). At the 11:45 dismissal the instructor runs the same Twilio console moment the 11:15 pickup-row names, on the instructor laptop on a dead-phone morning per the Day 3 dead-phone path: the day's pickup pings go out and that day's pre-scheduled crisis broadcast is cancelled before its 11:46 fire time, about one extra minute with the console already open, because the 11:15 pickup-row cancel never runs on a day the 11:15 slot becomes the showcase. The gallery strike stays unwound until the count hits zero and the cancel is confirmed: a day that ran normally sends nothing, and the broadcast fires only on a day the instructor never reaches the console, exactly the day it is needed. After dismissal the instructor returns the 3 rover kits to the labeled charging tray and strikes the gallery, about 20 minutes, working a named-home teardown checklist: the tape gun and badge tape go back in the Day 10 box, which is relocked in the storage closet with the other nine boxes; the 2 used 3x3 sticky-note pads go in the trash, one-session consumables like the scan-tab pads, with fresh ones packing at the next Session Zero Hour 2 off the Day 10 consumables row; the door table is cleared too: the combination-lock door-table lockbox and the sunscreen pump bottle go in the Day 10 box beside the tape gun before the closet relock, so the operator's move-out packs them with the ten day-boxes; the spent scan-tab sticky-note pads go in the trash, one-session consumables, fresh pads stage at the next Session Zero Hour 4; the spent door-table marker goes in the trash and the leftover paper stock sheets go in the paper recycling, the empty paper stock box alongside; the 4 markers return to the equipment shelf; the instructor collects the camper-kept fidget tools off the T-1-named camper's seat, wipes each with one of the alcohol-wipe packets the Day 10 prep staged at the instructor table, and stages the wiped tools in the labeled table-supply caddy on the equipment shelf, then pulls the 12 earplug pairs out of the caddy, wipes each pair with one more of the staged packets, and stages the wiped pairs back in the caddy, so the caddy the §08 move-out packs holds the full set the next session's Session Zero Hour 4 staging and Day 1 issue reach for; the interview-piece cart laptop (staged at the interview-piece capstone table in the Day 9 prep, carrying the two downloaded guest interviews) goes back on the laptop cart, plugged in to charge, so the move-out packs a whole cart, and it returns here, not earlier, because the laptop plays the interviews at its showcase station through the 11:45 dismissal; the instructor packs the 12 camp pens handed back at the 11:45 door dismissal back into the Day 1 box (bought once as reusable shop stock, so no re-buy; the box relocks in the closet with the other nine), so the next session's Session Zero Hour 2 packs the full set where the Day 1 prep reaches for it; the posted ship list and the unused spare ship list (kept in the Day 9 box at the instructor table since the Day 9 prep), leftover program printouts, the 12 panel scorecards (the award names were already copied onto the award cards in prep), the 24 peeled gallery labels, and the Day 2 grading sheet ("AI vs. you"), taken down from beside the grading station, and the two Tue-Thu print schedule cards, peeled off the printers, are recycled; the cards name this session's week-before AI-designed phone-stand files and the Tue-Thu print schedule, and the next session's week-before prompt produces two new design files, so the printers open the next session on bare panels; the laminated crew-rotation board and the laminated buddy-pair board come down off the lab wall beside the truth-rubric poster, where Day 1 prep hung them (they name this session's roster, so next session prints fresh ones at Session Zero Hour 1; laminated, they go in the trash, not the paper recycling); the written-on truth-rubric poster and the Wall of Almost poster board are one-per-session consumables, so both are recycled and a fresh pair is picked up at the next Session Zero copy-shop run, and a fresh grading sheet prints in the next Session Zero Hour 1 batch run on the venue laser printer; the 6 before-and-after pairs (each camper pair's bagged failed AI print beside its fixed human revision) stay on the museum shelf as the next session's seed exhibits, and any pairs older than one session are recycled, one phone photo per pair taken on the instructor's phone and saved into the session gallery folder on the instructor laptop first (this is what Day 5 prep's "clear the museum shelf for Friday's additions" clears); the 6 extra bagged failed prints (each pair's second AI print, the ones the 6 fixed revisions did not pair with) are recycled after the same phone-photo archive, so the shelf the next session's Day 5 prep clears holds exactly the 6 current pairs. Portfolios, journals, diplomas, and award cards already left the room with campers.

Every camper is counted, aloud, at every transition: room to room, room to field, field to room, room to pickup. The count is twelve. If the count is not twelve, nothing else happens until it is. At the final dismissal the count runs down, not up: the instructor counts from twelve to zero at sign-out, and the after-dismissal teardown steps in this schedule stay unwound until the count hits zero, so no day-box is relocked and no supply caddy returns to the shelf while a camper is still waiting.

The one-adult safety plan, in full.

Every procedure below is engineered for one adult and twelve campers, with no second adult at any moment. When a procedure cannot be done safely with one adult, the procedure is changed, never the staffing assumption.

  • Supervision modelThe instructor is in line-of-sight of all 12 campers every minute of the 60-minute lesson. Seating is a U around the work tables so no camper is behind the instructor. The 45-minute project block happens in the same room or at home, never in a second room that needs its own adult. Pickup: a camper leaves only with a person on the Airtable pickup-authorization list from enrollment; any change requires the parent to message the camp number, never a verbal relay through the child. Restroom: single-use restroom next to the main room; one camper at a time, the door visible from the instructor's seat; no pairs, no lines at the door, per the safety plan's venue restroom item below. Project block: the instructor stays in the room the whole 45 minutes doing silent admin (attendance, incident log; on Day 9, the showcase program master), no instruction, no minute unsupervised. Late arrivals are admitted at the next minute-block boundary on the parent's camp-number message, and early pickups are photo-ID-matched at the door with the instructor staying in the room; When a camper leaves mid-day, whether a scheduled early pickup or a same-day sick pickup, and that departure leaves the departing camper's buddy without a pair, the remaining camper joins the next present accountability pair in board order at the next minute-block boundary after the departure, skipping a pair that already hosts a mid-day substitute unless every present pair already hosts one, in which case the remaining camper joins the first present pair in board order, and that pair stands as the remaining camper's accountability pair for the rest of the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form. If the departing camper was the last other camper in the room, the remaining camper stays with the instructor as their accountability for the rest of the day, and no pair is named. When a camper who left on a scheduled early pickup returns later the same day, the return is admitted at the next minute-block boundary after the door-table check-in, with seating and a count re-run, or at the next quarter-hour mark inside the project block, and never at 11:15 pickup itself, which is dismissal, not check-in. A camper sent home sick does not return the same day. On return, the mid-day substitution unwinds: the returning camper rejoins their original buddy as their original pair, the host pair returns to its original two, and if the original buddy stayed with the instructor as a lone remainder, the buddy rejoins the returning camper as their original pair. If the original buddy has also left by the time of the return, the returning camper takes the instructor as their accountability for the rest of the day and counts as the next unpaired orphan if another camper arrives later. If that returning camper has since been paired with a late-arriving camper, because the returning camper counted as the next unpaired orphan, and the original buddy then returns later the same day, the buddy joins the returning camper and the late camper as an accountability trio for the rest of the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form, rather than displacing the late camper or splitting the pair; if the late camper later leaves mid-day, the original pair stands as the accountability pair and no further reseat is needed; if that late camper returns later the same day while the original pair still stands as the accountability pair, the trio re-forms for the rest of the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form, admitted under the return admission rules already in this clause, and if the original pair no longer stands when the late camper returns, the returning camper takes the instructor as their accountability for the rest of the day and counts as the next unpaired orphan if another camper arrives later; if a member of the original pair who left the trio mid-day returns later the same day while the remaining two still stand as the accountability pair, that returning member joins the remaining two as an accountability trio for the rest of the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form, rather than displacing the late camper or splitting the pair, admitted under the return admission rules already in this clause, and if the remaining two no longer stand as the accountability pair when that member returns, the returning member takes the instructor as their accountability for the rest of the day and counts as the next unpaired orphan if another camper arrives later; if either member of the original pair later leaves mid-day, the remaining two stand as the accountability pair for the rest of the day for the field walk, the dose gate, and the incident form, and no further reseat is needed. A return that arrives while the group is out on a field mission follows the same wait-or-take-home rule as a late arrival during a field mission, admitted at the first minute-block boundary after the group return count. A camper admitted during the project block joins the project corner with their restored pair, and the pair walks them through the handout header at the table before either resumes work. The laminated board itself is not reprinted for a mid-day substitution. The one-adult model holds at both ends of the day.
  • Soldering station (one, instructor-run)The solder station runs only while the instructor stands at it, hands on. The instructor is the only person who touches the hot iron; campers position pre-tinned wires and read the meter while the instructor makes the joint. Pairs rotate 5 minutes each, timed, the queue posted on the wall; six pairs fill the 30-minute build block exactly, matching the Day 4 run-of-show. Everyone else does non-heated work (wiring diagrams, code) at the tables. PPE: safety glasses on every face within arm's length, fume extractor on, the Day 4 burn kit within 10 feet of the station (the kit lives in the Day 4 box beside the soldering station and the Day 4 prep stages it beside the station; see the Day 4 materials list and the Session Zero Hour 4 staging). When the instructor steps away from the station, even one step, the iron goes back in the stand and the power switch goes off. At close, the station is locked in the Day 4 box, cord wrapped, before the project block starts.
  • Burn at the soldering station: iron down, cool, cover, the call line.
